A phase-by-phase roadmap with frameworks, worksheets, and actionable phase gates.<\/p>\n <div class=\"hero-rule\"><\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stats\">\n <div class=\"hero-stat\">\n <div class=\"hero-stat-num\">95%<\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stat-desc\">of enterprise AI pilots deliver no measurable P&L impact<\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stat-source\">MIT Project NANDA, July 2025<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stat\">\n <div class=\"hero-stat-num\">40%+<\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stat-desc\">of agentic AI projects predicted to be cancelled by end of 2027<\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stat-source\">Gartner, June 2025<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stat\">\n <div class=\"hero-stat-num\">$1T<\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stat-desc\">in SI services opportunity driven by agentic AI adoption<\/div>\n <div class=\"hero-stat-source\">Google Cloud \/ BCG, 2025<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- HOW TO USE + READER TRACKS -->\n<section class=\"section-cream\" id=\"how-to-use\">\n <div class=\"section-inner\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Before You Begin<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">How to Use This Playbook<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">This playbook serves two different readers. Identify which one you are before going further. Your track determines which sections are your primary outputs and which are reference material.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"track-grid\" style=\"margin-top:32px;\">\n <div class=\"track-card\">\n <span class=\"track-label\">Track A<\/span>\n <div class=\"track-title\">First-Time Builder<\/div>\n <p class=\"track-desc\">You have a use case and a rough idea. You have not built an agent before and need to understand what you’re building before you commit to building it.<\/p>\n <ul class=\"track-outputs\">\n <li>Read every section front to back<\/li>\n <li>The Build chapter is your primary output<\/li>\n <li>Complete all worksheets before moving to Build<\/li>\n <li>Use the First 48 Hours section as your literal starting point<\/li>\n <li style=\"border-top:1px solid var(--rule, rgba(0,0,0,.08)); padding-top:10px; margin-top:8px;\">Already have a use case? Browse <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/\">180+ pre-built agent blueprints<\/a> to skip the cold start.<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"track-card\">\n <span class=\"track-label\">Track B<\/span>\n <div class=\"track-title\">Experienced Builder, New Project<\/div>\n <p class=\"track-desc\">You know how to build agents. You need an organizational framework to align stakeholders, justify budget, and run the project without it stalling.<\/p>\n <ul class=\"track-outputs\">\n <li>Agent Design Canvas is your primary output<\/li>\n <li>Business Case Template for stakeholder alignment<\/li>\n <li>Phase Gate Checklists to prevent sequencing errors<\/li>\n <li>Build chapter is a reference, not a tutorial<\/li>\n <li style=\"border-top:1px solid var(--rule, rgba(0,0,0,.08)); padding-top:10px; margin-top:8px;\">Pair this with the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/playbook\/how-to-take-agents-to-production\/\">How to Take Agents to Production<\/a> playbook for your steering committee.<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n\n <div class=\"hr-amber\" style=\"margin-top:48px;\"><\/div>\n <h3 class=\"subsection\" style=\"margin-top:24px;\">Three Prerequisites<\/h3>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Before going further, answer these honestly. If you cannot answer all three, the relevant phases address each directly.<\/p>\n <ol class=\"prereq-list\">\n <li class=\"prereq-item\">\n <span class=\"prereq-num\">1<\/span>\n <p class=\"prereq-text\"><strong>Can you state your AI agent problem as a number?<\/strong> Not “improve customer service.” Something specific: “First-response time is 48 hours and needs to be under 4.”<\/p>\n <\/li>\n <li class=\"prereq-item\">\n <span class=\"prereq-num\">2<\/span>\n <p class=\"prereq-text\"><strong>Do you have one named person with P&L accountability who owns this?<\/strong> Not a committee. One person who can say yes to spend.<\/p>\n <\/li>\n <li class=\"prereq-item\">\n <span class=\"prereq-num\">3<\/span>\n <p class=\"prereq-text\"><strong>Do you know the difference between your current process and the ideal process if a human were not the bottleneck?<\/strong> If no, start at Phase 1 regardless of anything else.<\/p>\n <\/li>\n <\/ol>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- STATE OF AGENTS -->\n<section class=\"section-dark\" id=\"state\">\n <div class=\"section-inner\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow\">The State of AI Agents in 2026<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">The technology is not failing.<br>The approach is.<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\">There is a version of this moment told as a success story. AI agents are everywhere. Funding is flowing. Boards are demanding AI strategies. Here is the version that does not make the keynotes.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-trio\" style=\"margin-top:48px; max-width:900px; margin-left:auto; margin-right:auto;\">\n <div class=\"stat-card\">\n <div class=\"stat-card-num\">95%<\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-card-desc\">of enterprise AI pilots deliver no measurable P&L impact despite an estimated $30\u201340 billion in investment<\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-card-source\">MIT Project NANDA, July 2025<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-card\">\n <div class=\"stat-card-num\">42%<\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-card-desc\">of companies abandoned most AI initiatives in 2025, up from just 17% the year before<\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-card-source\">S&P Global, 2025<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-card\">\n <div class=\"stat-card-num\">23%<\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-card-desc\">of enterprises had actually integrated agentic AI into operations. 72\u201379% report adoption or testing. That gap is the opportunity.<\/div>\n <div class=\"stat-card-source\">McKinsey, State of AI 2025, November 2025<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"section-inner\" style=\"margin-top:48px;\">\n <hr class=\"hr-dark\">\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\">The teams that reach production are not smarter or better-funded. They are more structured. They ask different questions at the start. They phase their work in a specific order. They build governance before they need it.<\/p>\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\" style=\"margin-top:16px;\"><strong>The market opportunity is real.<\/strong> The global agentic AI market sits at $7.3\u20137.6 billion in 2025 and is projected to reach $139\u2013199 billion by 2034 (40\u201344% CAGR). Google Cloud and BCG have identified approximately $1 trillion in global systems integrator services driven by agentic AI adoption.<\/p>\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\" style=\"margin-top:16px; font-size:14px; opacity:.78;\">Why the technology works in demos and fails in production: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/multi-agent-vs-single-agent\/\">Multi-Agent vs Single-Agent<\/a> and <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/agentic-ai-vs-llm\/\">Agentic AI vs LLM<\/a> unpack the structural reasons.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- WHAT AN AGENT IS \u2014 PAMA\u041e (5 elements) -->\n<section class=\"section-cream-alt\">\n <div class=\"section-inner\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Foundations<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">What an AI Agent Actually Is<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Gartner estimates that of the thousands of vendors claiming agentic capabilities, only approximately 130 offer genuine agentic features. The rest are rebranded chatbots, RPA tools, and AI assistants, what Gartner calls “agent washing.” You cannot build a roadmap for something you cannot accurately define.<\/p>\n <p class=\"body-text\" style=\"margin-top:12px;\">A true AI agent has five capabilities. The first four define what it <em>is<\/em>. The fifth defines how it <em>works<\/em> in practice.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"pama-grid\">\n <div class=\"pama-card\">\n <div class=\"pama-letter\">P<\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-content\">\n <h4>Perception<\/h4>\n <p>Receives and interprets inputs from its environment: natural language, structured data, API responses, file contents, system events. A chatbot that only responds to typed text is not perceiving. It is matching patterns.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-card\">\n <div class=\"pama-letter\">A<\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-content\">\n <h4>Action<\/h4>\n <p>Takes actions that affect the world beyond generating text: calls APIs, updates databases, sends messages, executes code. An agent that only produces outputs for a human to act on is an assistant. An agent that acts directly is an agent.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-card\">\n <div class=\"pama-letter\">M<\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-content\">\n <h4>Memory<\/h4>\n <p>Retains relevant information across interactions: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/vector-database-by-lyzr-agent-studio\/\">short-term session memory, long-term persistent memory<\/a>, or domain knowledge in a retrieval layer. Without memory, every interaction starts from zero.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-card\">\n <div class=\"pama-letter\">A<\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-content\">\n <h4>Autonomy<\/h4>\n <p>Can pursue a goal across multiple steps without human input at each step. The level of autonomy varies. Autonomy without governance is a risk. Autonomy with governance is the goal.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-card pama-wide\">\n <div class=\"pama-letter\">O<\/div>\n <div class=\"pama-content\">\n <h4>Orchestration<\/h4>\n <p>The coordination layer that sequences multiple steps, routes between tools, handles conditional logic, and manages retries when something fails. Even a simple Level 1 agent typically has at least two steps: an input parsing step and an output generation step. Orchestration is what connects them. Without it, you do not have an agent. You have a prompt with extra steps. This is the component most first-time builders underestimate, and the one most responsible for agents that work in demos but break in production. Deep dive: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/agent-orchestration\/\">agent orchestration<\/a> and <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/multi-agent-architecture\/\">multi-agent architecture patterns<\/a>.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- 5-PHASE FRAMEWORK -->\n<section class=\"section-dark\" id=\"framework\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow\">The Roadmap<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">The 5-Phase Framework<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\">Most agent projects fail because teams do things in the wrong order. They build before validating the problem. They deploy before designing governance. They scale before a single agent has proven value. Each phase produces something the next phase depends on. Skip one and the dependency breaks.<\/p>\n <div class=\"phase-strip\">\n <a class=\"phase-cell\" href=\"#phase1\" style=\"text-decoration:none; color:inherit; display:block;\"><span class=\"phase-num\">01<\/span><div class=\"phase-name\">Discovery<\/div><div class=\"phase-desc\">Quantified problem, named champion, locked use case. Prevents hype-driven selection.<\/div><\/a>\n <a class=\"phase-cell\" href=\"#phase2\" style=\"text-decoration:none; color:inherit; display:block;\"><span class=\"phase-num\">02<\/span><div class=\"phase-name\">Architecture<\/div><div class=\"phase-desc\">Agent Design Canvas, Integration Map, SLOs, governance design. Prevents integration surprises.<\/div><\/a>\n <a class=\"phase-cell\" href=\"#phase3\" style=\"text-decoration:none; color:inherit; display:block;\"><span class=\"phase-num\">03<\/span><div class=\"phase-name\">Build<\/div><div class=\"phase-desc\">Level 1 agent: one trigger, one flow, one output. Prevents over-scoping and untested handoffs.<\/div><\/a>\n <a class=\"phase-cell\" href=\"#govern\" style=\"text-decoration:none; color:inherit; display:block;\"><span class=\"phase-num\">04<\/span><div class=\"phase-name\">Deploy & Govern<\/div><div class=\"phase-desc\">Production agent with RBAC, audit, cost caps, monitoring live from day one.<\/div><\/a>\n <a class=\"phase-cell\" href=\"#scale\" style=\"text-decoration:none; color:inherit; display:block;\"><span class=\"phase-num\">05<\/span><div class=\"phase-name\">Scale<\/div><div class=\"phase-desc\">Level 2 multi-agent architecture only after Level 1 proves value in production.<\/div><\/a>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- WHY PROJECTS FAIL \u2014 NOW WITH DIAGNOSTICS -->\n<section class=\"section-cream\" id=\"failures\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Root Causes<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Why Agent Projects Fail:<br>The 5 Structural Root Causes<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Each failure mode includes a self-diagnostic. Run these against your current situation before you start. The “Problem” column tells you what goes wrong. The “Diagnostic” column tells you whether it is already going wrong for you.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"failure-list\">\n <div class=\"failure-card\">\n <div class=\"failure-num\">01<\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-problem\">\n <div class=\"failure-title\">Hype-Driven Selection<\/div>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">Organizations commit budget because leadership read an article or saw a competitor announcement, without identifying a specific problem with a measurable cost. Gartner: “Most agentic AI projects right now are driven by hype and are often misapplied.”<\/p>\n <div class=\"failure-fix\" style=\"margin-top:16px; padding:0; border:none; background:none;\">\n <span class=\"fix-label\">The Fix<\/span>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">The business problem must be stated as a number before any technology is touched. Not “improve customer service.” Something like: “First-response time is 48 hours and needs to be under 4.” For a structured selection method, see the 4-quadrant matrix in our <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/playbook\/agentforce-alternative\/\">Agentforce alternative playbook<\/a>.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-diag\">\n <span class=\"diag-label\">Self-Diagnostic<\/span>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Can you name the specific person whose job gets measurably easier if this works?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Can you state the current cost of the problem in hours or dollars per month?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Has someone said “we should do something with AI” without a specific process in mind?<\/p>\n <p class=\"failure-body\" style=\"margin-top:12px; font-size:12.5px; color:var(--text-muted);\">If you answered no, no, yes: you are in hype-driven territory. Do not proceed to Architecture until you have a number.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-card\">\n <div class=\"failure-num\">02<\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-problem\">\n <div class=\"failure-title\">Automating a Broken Process<\/div>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">Organizations that succeed are more than twice as likely to have redesigned their workflows before selecting technology (MIT NANDA, 2025). Agentic AI does not improve a broken process. It automates it. The broken parts run faster and create problems at higher volume.<\/p>\n <div class=\"failure-fix\" style=\"margin-top:16px; padding:0; border:none; background:none;\">\n <span class=\"fix-label\">The Fix<\/span>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">Map the current state. Design the ideal state assuming no human bottleneck. Build the agent for the redesigned process, not the existing one. <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/case-studies\/hfs-research\/\">How HFS rebuilt their research workflow before automating it<\/a> is the cleanest worked example.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-diag\">\n <span class=\"diag-label\">Self-Diagnostic<\/span>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Can you draw the current process on a whiteboard, every step, every handoff, every system?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Are there steps in the current process that exist only because of human limitations (scheduling, manual lookup, copy-paste)?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Does the process produce consistent outputs, or does it depend heavily on who is doing it?<\/p>\n <p class=\"failure-body\" style=\"margin-top:12px; font-size:12.5px; color:var(--text-muted);\">If you cannot draw the current process, or if it only works when specific people do it: redesign first. Build second.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-card\">\n <div class=\"failure-num\">03<\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-problem\">\n <div class=\"failure-title\">Governance as an Afterthought<\/div>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">Gartner names three causes for its 40%+ cancellation prediction: escalating costs, unclear business value, and inadequate risk controls. All three are governance failures. Costs escalate without cost architecture. Value is unclear without SLOs. Risk controls fail when RBAC is bolted on after the fact.<\/p>\n <div class=\"failure-fix\" style=\"margin-top:16px; padding:0; border:none; background:none;\">\n <span class=\"fix-label\">The Fix<\/span>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">RBAC, audit trails, cost caps, and approval gates are designed in Phase 2, not added after deployment. Governance is not friction. It is how agents earn the organizational trust needed to expand. See <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/governance-in-aws\/\">Governance in AWS<\/a> and Lyzr’s <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/responsible-ai-as-a-service\/\">Responsible AI as a Service<\/a> layer.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-diag\">\n <span class=\"diag-label\">Self-Diagnostic<\/span>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Do you know what a failed agent run will cost you in LLM tokens?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Have you defined what “working correctly” means as a number, before writing any code?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">If the agent sends a wrong message to a customer tomorrow, who finds out and how?<\/p>\n <p class=\"failure-body\" style=\"margin-top:12px; font-size:12.5px; color:var(--text-muted);\">If you cannot answer all three, governance is not designed. Fill in the Phase 2 canvas before starting Build.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-card\">\n <div class=\"failure-num\">04<\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-problem\">\n <div class=\"failure-title\">Underestimating Integration<\/div>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">70% of developers cite integration problems as a primary challenge. 42% of enterprises need access to 8+ data sources to deploy agents successfully, with 79% expecting data challenges to impact rollouts. An agent with a perfect prompt but unreliable data retrieval is not a production agent.<\/p>\n <div class=\"failure-fix\" style=\"margin-top:16px; padding:0; border:none; background:none;\">\n <span class=\"fix-label\">The Fix<\/span>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">Integration mapping is Phase 2 work, not Phase 3. Every data source is identified, access is confirmed, and auth is resolved before the first line of agent code is written. <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/mcp-in-lyzr-agent-studio\/\">MCP in Lyzr Agent Studio<\/a> explains how to map 8+ data sources without per-agent integration code.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-diag\">\n <span class=\"diag-label\">Self-Diagnostic<\/span>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Can you list every system the agent needs to read from or write to?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">For each system, do you have confirmed API access, or are you assuming you can get it?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Do any of those systems require IT procurement approval that has not started?<\/p>\n <p class=\"failure-body\" style=\"margin-top:12px; font-size:12.5px; color:var(--text-muted);\">Assumed access is not confirmed access. If any system says “we think we can get that,” stop and verify before scoping the build.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-card\">\n <div class=\"failure-num\">05<\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-problem\">\n <div class=\"failure-title\">No Named Champion<\/div>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">Mid-market companies move from pilot to production in an average of 90 days. Large enterprises average 9 months or more. The difference is not resources. It is decision authority (MIT NANDA, 2025). Without someone with P&L accountability invested in the outcome, the first real obstacle pauses the project permanently.<\/p>\n <div class=\"failure-fix\" style=\"margin-top:16px; padding:0; border:none; background:none;\">\n <span class=\"fix-label\">The Fix<\/span>\n <p class=\"failure-body\">Before scoping begins: name one person who can say yes to spend without a committee, who feels the cost of the problem in their own metrics, and who will still care in 90 days. <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/playbook\/bfsi-guide-to-dispute-management\/\">The BFSI Dispute Management Playbook<\/a> shows a real champion-led rollout from scope to production.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"failure-diag\">\n <span class=\"diag-label\">Self-Diagnostic<\/span>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">If the project hits a real obstacle in month two, is there one specific person who will fight to keep it moving?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Does that person’s team directly feel the pain of the problem this agent solves?<\/p>\n <p class=\"diag-q\">Can that person approve spend without going to a committee?<\/p>\n <p class=\"failure-body\" style=\"margin-top:12px; font-size:12.5px; color:var(--text-muted);\">Three yeses = you have a champion. Anything less = you have enthusiasm, not ownership. Do not start scoping without a champion.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- PHASE 1 -->\n<section class=\"section-dark\" id=\"phase1\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <div class=\"phase-badge\"><span class=\"phase-badge-dot\"><\/span> Phase 01 \u00b7 Discovery<\/div>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">The Champion-Budget-Scope<br>Framework<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\">Before any agent is scoped, before any tool is selected, before any prompt is written: three things must be true. If any one of them is missing, the project will either never start or never finish.<\/p>\n <div class=\"cbs-grid\">\n <div class=\"cbs-card\">\n <span class=\"cbs-label\">Champion<\/span>\n <div class=\"cbs-content\">\n <div class=\"cbs-item\"><h5>What it is<\/h5><p>One named person with P&L accountability who owns the outcome and can approve spend without a committee.<\/p><\/div>\n <div class=\"cbs-item\"><h5>What it is NOT<\/h5><p>A senior person who is “supportive.” A team that is “interested.” A steering group that will “review progress.”<\/p><\/div>\n <div class=\"cbs-item\">\n <h5>Red Flags<\/h5>\n <ul class=\"cbs-flags\">\n <li>“The team is very excited about this”<\/li>\n <li>Sponsor changes quarterly<\/li>\n <li>No single name when asked who owns it<\/li>\n <li>Champion’s team is not the primary user<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"cbs-card\">\n <span class=\"cbs-label\">Budget<\/span>\n <div class=\"cbs-content\">\n <div class=\"cbs-item\"><h5>The Conversation to Have<\/h5><p>Ask the champion directly: “Is this in your current FY budget or does it need a new approval?” In budget: proceed. Needs approval, champion can give it: proceed with timeline. Needs approval above champion: you need a co-sponsor.<\/p><\/div>\n <div class=\"cbs-item\">\n <h5>Cost Range for First Agent<\/h5>\n <ul class=\"cbs-flags\">\n <li>Internal developer: $15K\u201345K (3\u20136 weeks, 1\u20132 devs)<\/li>\n <li>External implementation: $25K\u201375K<\/li>\n <li>Platform infrastructure: $500\u20133K\/month ongoing<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"cbs-item\"><p>A project without a committed budget number is a conversation, not a project.<\/p><\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"cbs-card\">\n <span class=\"cbs-label\">Scope<\/span>\n <div class=\"cbs-content\">\n <div class=\"cbs-item\">\n <h5>The Test<\/h5>\n <p><strong style=\"color:var(--amber);\">YES:<\/strong> “Our support team handles 2,400 tier-1 tickets per month. 68% require no human judgment. We want an agent to resolve that 68% without escalation.”<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"cbs-item\"><p><strong style=\"color:rgba(240,232,228,.4);\">NO:<\/strong> “We want to use AI to improve customer experience.”<\/p><\/div>\n <div class=\"cbs-item\">\n <h5>First Scope Sits Where<\/h5>\n <ul class=\"cbs-flags\">\n <li>Current-state cost is measurable<\/li>\n <li>Agent handles a meaningful % without human judgment<\/li>\n <li>Data is accessible (not locked in legacy systems)<\/li>\n <li>Failure is visible and recoverable, not catastrophic<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- DISCOVERY CONVERSATION -->\n<section class=\"section-cream\">\n <div class=\"section-inner\">\n <div class=\"phase-badge\" style=\"background:var(--bg-cream-alt); border:1px solid var(--rule);\"><span class=\"phase-badge-dot\" style=\"background:var(--amber-dark);\"><\/span><span style=\"color:var(--text-mid);\">Phase 01 \u00b7 Discovery, Continued<\/span><\/div>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">The 30-Minute Discovery Conversation<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Run this with your champion before anything else. These questions separate real projects from wishful thinking. Take notes. The answers are the inputs to your business case.<\/p>\n <table class=\"conv-table\">\n <tr><td>Problem<\/td><td>“If this agent works perfectly, which number in your business changes, and by how much?”<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Current cost<\/td><td>“How many hours per month does your team spend on this today? What is the error rate? What is the escalation volume?”<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Data access<\/td><td>“What systems hold the data this agent would need? Do you have API access or would that require IT approval?”<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Timeline<\/td><td>“What does success look like in 30 days? 90 days? Is there a business event this needs to land before?”<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Constraints<\/td><td>“What would stop this? Who in the organization would push back, and what would they say?”<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Measure<\/td><td>“How will we know, on a Tuesday afternoon three months from now, whether this was worth doing?”<\/td><\/tr>\n <\/table>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- USE CASE SELECTION -->\n<section class=\"section-cream-alt\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 01 \u00b7 Discovery<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Use Case Selection &<br>The Opportunity Matrix<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Start with a structured inventory of where repetitive, structured, high-volume work already exists.<\/p>\n\n <h3 class=\"subsection\">Where to Look: The Six Categories<\/h3>\n <p class=\"body-text\" style=\"margin-top:8px; font-size:13.5px;\">Each row links to a working blueprint. <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/\">Browse the full blueprint library<\/a> for industry-specific starting points.<\/p>\n <table class=\"signal-table\" style=\"margin-top:20px;\">\n <thead><tr><th>Category<\/th><th>Agent-Ready Examples<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>\n <tbody>\n <tr><td>Customer Operations<\/td><td><a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/customer-service\/ai-customer-support-agent\/\">Tier-1 support tickets<\/a>, FAQ resolution, returns processing, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/customer-service\/customer-onboarding-agent\/\">onboarding steps that follow a decision tree<\/a><\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Finance & Compliance<\/td><td><a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/finance\/invoice-matching-agent\/\">Invoice matching<\/a>, expense categorization, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/finance\/cash-application-agent\/\">reconciliation<\/a>, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/legal\/internal-audit-checklist-agent\/\">audit trail generation<\/a>, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/banking\/kyc-compliance-agent\/\">KYC checks<\/a><\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Sales & GTM<\/td><td><a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/procurement\/qualification-scoring-agent\/\">Lead qualification scoring<\/a>, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/sales\/prospect-research-agent\/\">outbound research<\/a>, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/sales\/lead-enrichment-agent\/\">CRM data enrichment<\/a>, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/sales\/ai-proposal-writer-agent\/\">proposal generation<\/a><\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>HR & Internal Ops<\/td><td><a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/hr\/hr-helpdesk-agent\/\">Employee FAQ handling<\/a>, onboarding document routing, leave request processing, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/hr\/policy-generator-agent\/\">policy lookups<\/a><\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Data & Reporting<\/td><td><a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/finance\/finance-analysis-reporting-agent\/\">Weekly report generation<\/a>, data normalization, dashboard population, alert triage<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Supply Chain & Ops<\/td><td>Inventory status queries, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blueprints\/finance\/supplier-onboarding-agent\/\">supplier onboarding and communication<\/a>, shipment tracking, exception flagging<\/td><\/tr>\n <\/tbody>\n <\/table>\n\n <h3 class=\"subsection\">The 4-Quadrant Opportunity Matrix<\/h3>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Plot your candidates. Two axes: <strong>Business Impact<\/strong> (what does this cost today, or what does it unlock?) and <strong>Implementation Complexity<\/strong> (data accessibility, integration count, governance requirements).<\/p>\n <div class=\"matrix\" style=\"margin-top:28px;\">\n <div class=\"matrix-axis-label\" style=\"grid-row:1\/3; grid-column:1;\">High Complexity \u2191 Low Complexity<\/div>\n <div class=\"matrix-cell cell-plan\" style=\"grid-row:1; grid-column:2;\"><h5>Plan for Phase 2<\/h5><p>High value but too complex for a first build. Document it. Revisit after Level 1 proves value.<\/p><\/div>\n <div class=\"matrix-cell cell-build\" style=\"grid-row:1; grid-column:3;\"><h5>Build First \u2713<\/h5><p>Clear value + achievable scope. This is your first agent. Quantify the baseline cost today.<\/p><\/div>\n <div class=\"matrix-cell cell-skip\" style=\"grid-row:2; grid-column:2;\"><h5>Skip<\/h5><p>Neither the value nor the complexity justifies the distraction. Leave it off the roadmap.<\/p><\/div>\n <div class=\"matrix-cell cell-win\" style=\"grid-row:2; grid-column:3;\"><h5>Consider for Quick Win<\/h5><p>Low complexity, lower returns. Only pursue if you need an early internal demonstration of value.<\/p><\/div>\n <div style=\"grid-row:3; grid-column:2;\" class=\"matrix-bottom-label\">Low Impact<\/div>\n <div style=\"grid-row:3; grid-column:3;\" class=\"matrix-bottom-label\">High Impact \u2192<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- USE CASE SCORECARD -->\n<section class=\"section-cream\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 01 \u00b7 Discovery: Worksheet<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Use Case Scoring Worksheet<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Score your top 3\u20134 use cases across 5 dimensions. Each dimension scored 1\u20135. Maximum 25 points. The use case with the highest score AND a committed champion is your first build. Score \u226518 with a committed champion = proceed to Phase 2.<\/p>\n <table class=\"score-table\">\n <thead><tr><th>Dimension<\/th><th>What It Measures<\/th><th style=\"text-align:center;\">Use Case A<br>\/5<\/th><th style=\"text-align:center;\">Use Case B<br>\/5<\/th><th style=\"text-align:center;\">Use Case C<br>\/5<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>\n <tbody>\n <tr><td>Measurable Cost<\/td><td>Can you quantify what this costs today in time, errors, or revenue impact? (1=no data, 5=precise numbers)<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Data Accessibility<\/td><td>Is the required data in accessible systems with existing APIs? (1=locked legacy, 5=clean API ready)<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Agent Coverage<\/td><td>What % of total volume can the agent handle without human judgment? (1=<20%, 5=>70%)<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Low Governance Risk<\/td><td>Is failure recoverable? Can humans review before permanent action? (1=irreversible\/public, 5=internal\/reversible)<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Champion Commitment<\/td><td>Does your champion personally feel this problem and own the outcome? (1=indirect, 5=primary pain owner)<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><td class=\"score-input\">__ \/5<\/td><\/tr>\n <\/tbody>\n <tfoot><tr><td><strong>Total Score<\/strong><\/td><td style=\"color:var(--amber);\">Maximum: 25 points<\/td><td class=\"score-total\">__ \/25<\/td><td class=\"score-total\">__ \/25<\/td><td class=\"score-total\">__ \/25<\/td><\/tr><\/tfoot>\n <\/table>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- PHASE 2 -->\n<section class=\"section-cream-alt\" id=\"phase2\">\n <div class=\"section-inner\">\n <div class=\"phase-badge\" style=\"background:var(--bg-dark);\"><span class=\"phase-badge-dot\"><\/span> Phase 02 \u00b7 Architecture<\/div>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Building Your Business Case<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">A business case is not a slide deck with market projections. It is a one-page document that answers three questions: what does this cost today, what will the agent change, and when do we break even? If you cannot answer all three, you are not ready to build.<\/p>\n <p class=\"body-text\" style=\"margin-top:8px;\">Two annotated, real-world business cases that map directly to the table below: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/how-hfs-used-lyzr-to-build-an-agentic-system-for-enterprise-research\/\">HFS Research<\/a> and the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/case-studies\/accenture-accubate\/\">Accenture \/ Accubate case study<\/a>. ROI baseline math: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/template\/performance-roi-calculator\/\">Performance ROI Calculator<\/a>.<\/p>\n <table class=\"bc-table\">\n <tr><td>Current-state baseline<\/td><td>Volume handled per month \u00d7 average handling time \u00d7 fully-loaded cost per hour = Monthly cost before agent<\/td><td>$______<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Agent-handled volume<\/td><td>Total volume \u00d7 coverage % (use conservative estimate)<\/td><td>___ units<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Cost per intervention<\/td><td>Remaining human-handled cases \u00d7 handling time \u00d7 hourly cost + agent infrastructure cost per month<\/td><td>$______<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Gross monthly savings<\/td><td>Current-state baseline minus Cost per intervention (agent-handled + remaining human)<\/td><td>$______<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Implementation cost<\/td><td>All-in: development, infrastructure setup, integration work, testing. Typical range: $25K\u201375K<\/td><td>$______<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Break-even timeline<\/td><td>Implementation cost \u00f7 Gross monthly savings = break-even months. Target: <6 months for first agent<\/td><td>__ months<\/td><\/tr>\n <\/table>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- COST ESTIMATION + INTEGRATION PATTERNS -->\n<section class=\"section-cream\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 02 \u00b7 Architecture: Technical Grounding<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">LLM Cost Estimation &<br>Integration Patterns<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Two things that consistently surprise first-time builders: how much LLM calls cost at real volume, and how long integration confirmation actually takes. Both need to be in your business case and your timeline before Build starts.<\/p>\n\n <h3 class=\"subsection\">Approximate LLM Cost at 1,000 Queries Per Month<\/h3>\n <p class=\"body-text\">These are order-of-magnitude estimates for a standard support agent handling queries of 200\u2013500 tokens each, with a response of similar length. Actual costs vary significantly by prompt length, response length, and task complexity.<\/p>\n <table class=\"cost-table\">\n <thead><tr><th>Model<\/th><th>Provider<\/th><th>Approx. Monthly Cost<\/th><th>Best For<\/th><th>Trade-off<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>\n <tbody>\n <tr><td>GPT-4o mini<\/td><td>OpenAI<\/td><td><span class=\"cost-badge cost-low\">$2\u20138<\/span><\/td><td>Simple classification, FAQ, routing<\/td><td>Lower reasoning quality on complex tasks<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Claude Haiku 3.5<\/td><td>Anthropic<\/td><td><span class=\"cost-badge cost-low\">$2\u20136<\/span><\/td><td>Document processing, structured extraction<\/td><td>Less capable on open-ended generation<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>GPT-4o<\/td><td>OpenAI<\/td><td><span class=\"cost-badge cost-med\">$25\u201380<\/span><\/td><td>Complex reasoning, multi-step workflows<\/td><td>Cost grows fast at high volume<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Claude Sonnet 4.5<\/td><td>Anthropic<\/td><td><span class=\"cost-badge cost-med\">$20\u201370<\/span><\/td><td>Analysis, long documents, nuanced tasks<\/td><td>Cost grows fast at high volume<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Llama 3.1 (self-hosted)<\/td><td>Meta \/ your infra<\/td><td><span class=\"cost-badge cost-low\">Infra only<\/span><\/td><td>Sensitive data, high volume, cost control<\/td><td>Requires engineering to deploy and maintain<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>GPT-4o (10K queries)<\/td><td>OpenAI<\/td><td><span class=\"cost-badge cost-high\">$250\u2013800<\/span><\/td><td>Same as above, 10x volume<\/td><td>Choose a cheaper model or self-host first<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr style=\"background:var(--bg-cream-deep); border-top:2px solid var(--rule);\">\n <td style=\"color:var(--text-dark); font-weight:700;\">LyzrGPT (platform)<\/td>\n <td style=\"color:var(--text-mid);\">Lyzr \u00b7 multi-model<\/td>\n <td><span class=\"cost-badge cost-low\">$0.03\u20130.08\/run<br>+ LLM cost<\/span><\/td>\n <td>Teams that want automatic model routing without managing separate API contracts per provider<\/td>\n <td>Platform fee on top of underlying LLM cost. Best value above 5K runs\/month where routing savings offset the platform layer.<\/td>\n <\/tr>\n <\/tbody>\n <\/table>\n <p class=\"cost-note\">Rule of thumb: start with the cheapest model that passes your quality test. For most first agents, GPT-4o mini or Claude Haiku handles the task adequately and costs 10\u201320x less than flagship models at the same volume.<\/p>\n\n <h3 class=\"subsection\">Three Integration Patterns to Know Before You Build<\/h3>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Every first agent involves at least one integration. Here are the three patterns that cover 80% of cases, what you need to confirm before Build starts, and what breaks in production when you skip the verification step.<\/p>\n <div class=\"integration-grid\">\n <div class=\"integ-card\">\n <span class=\"integ-type\">Pattern 01<\/span>\n <div class=\"integ-title\">REST API with Auth Token<\/div>\n <p class=\"integ-body\">The most common pattern. The agent calls an external API (CRM, ticketing system, database service) using a key or OAuth token.<\/p>\n <ul class=\"integ-items\">\n <li>What you need before Build: API documentation, a test API key, and a confirmed sandbox environment<\/li>\n <li>Typical timeline to confirm: 1\u20135 days if IT owns the API<\/li>\n <li>What breaks in production: tokens expire (usually 30\u201390 days) without automatic refresh logic. Auth breaks silently and the agent fails without a clear error.<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <div class=\"integ-warning\">Confirm token expiry policy and build refresh logic before go-live, not after.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"integ-card\">\n <span class=\"integ-type\">Pattern 02<\/span>\n <div class=\"integ-title\">Database with Read-Only Service Account<\/div>\n <p class=\"integ-body\">The agent queries a SQL or NoSQL database directly. Most common for internal reporting tasks, data lookup, or enrichment tasks.<\/p>\n <ul class=\"integ-items\">\n <li>What you need before Build: a read-only service account, confirmed schema access, and a test query that returns real data<\/li>\n <li>Typical timeline to confirm: 1\u20133 weeks if DBA approval is required<\/li>\n <li>What breaks in production: schema changes in the source database break agent queries with no warning. Add schema monitoring to your SLO check.<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <div class=\"integ-warning\">DBA approval cycles are the most common cause of Build delays. Start the request in Phase 2, not Phase 3.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"integ-card\">\n <span class=\"integ-type\">Pattern 03<\/span>\n <div class=\"integ-title\">File-Based Input via Document Upload<\/div>\n <p class=\"integ-body\">The agent processes uploaded documents (PDFs, CSVs, DOCX). Most common for document review tasks, contract analysis, or report generation tasks.<\/p>\n <ul class=\"integ-items\">\n <li>What you need before Build: sample documents in production format, confirmed file size limits, and a clear understanding of the document’s internal structure<\/li>\n <li>Typical timeline to confirm: 1\u20132 days<\/li>\n <li>What breaks in production: real documents are messier than sample documents. Tables, embedded images, and scanned PDFs break extraction logic that worked perfectly in testing.<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <div class=\"integ-warning\">Test with your 10 messiest real documents, not your 10 cleanest sample files.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div style=\"margin-top:16px; padding:18px 22px; background:var(--bg-cream-deep); border-left:3px solid var(--amber-dark); display:grid; grid-template-columns:auto 1fr; gap:16px; align-items:start;\">\n <span style=\"font-family:var(--mono); font-size:9px; letter-spacing:.15em; text-transform:uppercase; color:var(--amber-dark); font-weight:700; padding-top:2px; white-space:nowrap;\">On Architect<\/span>\n <p style=\"font-size:13.5px; color:var(--text-body); line-height:1.65;\">All three integration patterns are handled through native tool connectivity. You authenticate once per integration at the platform level. Token refresh, schema-change monitoring, and document preprocessing are managed by the platform rather than built per agent \u2014 which removes the most common single cause of production failures from your Build scope entirely. Start at <a href=\"https:\/\/architect.new\" style=\"color:var(--text-mid); font-weight:600;\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\">architect.new<\/a>.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <div style=\"margin-top:8px; padding:18px 22px; background:var(--bg-cream-deep); border-left:3px solid var(--amber-dark); display:grid; grid-template-columns:auto 1fr; gap:16px; align-items:start;\">\n <span style=\"font-family:var(--mono); font-size:9px; letter-spacing:.15em; text-transform:uppercase; color:var(--amber-dark); font-weight:700; padding-top:2px; white-space:nowrap;\">For Enterprise<\/span>\n <p style=\"font-size:13.5px; color:var(--text-body); line-height:1.65;\">For SSO, multi-cloud deployment, and 8+ data-source orchestration with full governance baked in: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/lyzr-agent-studio\/\" style=\"color:var(--text-mid); font-weight:600;\">Lyzr Agent Studio<\/a>. See it on your stack \u2014 <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/book-demo\/?utm_source=roadmap&utm_medium=inline_callout&utm_campaign=integration_studio\" style=\"color:var(--text-mid); font-weight:600;\">book a demo<\/a>.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n<section class=\"section-cream-alt\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 02 \u00b7 Architecture: Worksheet<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">The Agent Design Canvas<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Complete all 9 sections before writing any code. If you cannot fill in a section, that section is your next task, not something to figure out during Build. An incomplete canvas is a risk register. The right column shows a worked example for a tier-1 support agent.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"canvas-grid\">\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">01<\/span><h5>Goal Statement<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">One sentence: what does this agent do, for whom, and how is success measured?<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>Resolve tier-1 support tickets for SaaS customers without human intervention. Success: 65%+ resolution rate with <3% error rate.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">02<\/span><h5>Trigger<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">What event starts the agent? (Incoming message \/ scheduled time \/ API event \/ manual)<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>Incoming support ticket via API webhook from Zendesk. Fires on every new ticket tagged “tier-1”.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">03<\/span><h5>Inputs<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">What data does the agent receive? Where does it come from? What format?<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>Ticket body (plain text), customer ID, account tier. Source: Zendesk webhook JSON. Also retrieves account history from CRM via REST API call.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">04<\/span><h5>Actions<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">What does the agent do? List every action it can take: API calls, DB writes, messages sent.<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>1. Query knowledge base for relevant articles. 2. Draft response using retrieved context. 3. Post reply to Zendesk ticket. 4. Mark ticket as resolved or escalate tag.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">05<\/span><h5>Tools & Integrations<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">Every external system. Confirm API access and auth method for each.<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>Zendesk API (OAuth, confirmed). Internal knowledge base (REST API, read-only key, confirmed). CRM (service account, DBA approval pending).<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">06<\/span><h5>Memory Requirements<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">Short-term (session only) or long-term (persist across sessions)? What must it remember?<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>Short-term only for v1. Agent holds ticket context within a single session. Long-term (customer history retrieval) is Phase 2 scope.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">07<\/span><h5>Handoff Conditions<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">When does the agent escalate to a human? Under what conditions does it stop?<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>Escalate if: ticket contains billing dispute keyword, customer is enterprise tier, confidence score below 0.7, or agent has attempted 2 responses with no resolution.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">08<\/span><h5>SLO (Success Metric)<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">Numbers: error rate \u2264 X%, response latency \u2264 Y seconds, volume threshold \u2265 Z\/day.<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>Error rate \u2264 3%. Latency \u2264 8 seconds P95. Resolution rate \u2265 65% of assigned tickets. Volume: handles 80+ tickets\/day without degradation.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-row\">\n <div class=\"canvas-section-label\"><span class=\"num\">09<\/span><h5>Failure Mode<\/h5><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-guide\">What does a bad outcome look like? Is it reversible? Who is notified?<\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-example\"><strong>Example<\/strong>Agent sends incorrect resolution. Reversible (customer can reopen). Alert fires to support lead via Slack within 5 minutes of error detection. Weekly error report to champion.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-sign\">\n <div class=\"canvas-sign-field\"><label>Champion Sign-Off<\/label><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-sign-field\"><label>Project Lead Sign-Off<\/label><\/div>\n <div class=\"canvas-sign-field\"><label>Date<\/label><\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- PHASE 3 BUILD \u2014 FULL REBUILD -->\n<section class=\"section-dark\" id=\"phase3\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <div class=\"phase-badge\"><span class=\"phase-badge-dot\"><\/span> Phase 03 \u00b7 Build<\/div>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Building Your First Agent<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\">Build starts only after the Agent Design Canvas is complete and signed. Everything in Phase 2 was the work that makes Build predictable. This chapter covers what to build with, how to choose a model, how the components fit together, and what to do on your literal first day.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- MODEL SELECTION -->\n<section class=\"section-cream-alt\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 03 \u00b7 Build: Model Selection<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Choosing Your Model<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">The most consequential early decision is which model to use. The right answer depends on your task type, your volume, and your cost constraints, not on which model sounds most impressive. Start with the cheapest model that passes your quality test. Upgrade only when you can measure the quality gap.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"model-tree\" style=\"margin-top:28px;\">\n <div class=\"model-row model-header\">\n <div class=\"model-task\">Task Type<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-reason\">Reasoning<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-pick\">Default Model<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"model-row\">\n <div class=\"model-task\">Classification, routing, simple FAQ<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-reason\">Pattern-matching tasks. Response quality difference between cheap and expensive models is minimal. Cost difference is 10\u201320x.<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-pick\">GPT-4o mini or Claude Haiku<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"model-row\">\n <div class=\"model-task\">Structured data extraction from documents<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-reason\">Consistent output format matters more than reasoning depth. Cheaper models handle this well when the prompt is precise.<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-pick\">Claude Haiku or GPT-4o mini<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"model-row\">\n <div class=\"model-task\">Multi-step reasoning, complex analysis<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-reason\">Tasks where the model needs to hold multiple variables, weigh trade-offs, or follow complex instructions benefit from a stronger model.<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-pick\">GPT-4o or Claude Sonnet<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"model-row\">\n <div class=\"model-task\">Long document processing (>50 pages)<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-reason\">Context window size becomes the constraint. You need a model with a large, reliable context window, not just a large one.<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-pick\">Gemini 1.5 Pro or Claude Sonnet<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"model-row\">\n <div class=\"model-task\">Sensitive data (PII, healthcare, finance)<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-reason\">Data must not leave your infrastructure. Self-hosted open-source models are the only option that guarantees this at the infrastructure level.<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-pick\">Llama 3.1 (self-hosted)<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"model-row\">\n <div class=\"model-task\">High-volume production (>50K queries\/month)<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-reason\">At this volume, model cost dominates your infrastructure cost. A 10x cost reduction per query is worth significant engineering investment.<\/div>\n <div class=\"model-pick\">Self-hosted or fine-tuned cheaper model<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <p style=\"margin-top:16px; font-size:13px; color:var(--text-muted); font-style:italic;\">If you are unsure: start with GPT-4o mini or Claude Haiku for your first build. Run 50 real test cases. If quality is insufficient, upgrade to GPT-4o or Claude Sonnet. The quality gap is usually smaller than expected, and the cost gap is always larger.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- TOOL CHOICE -->\n<section class=\"section-cream\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 03 \u00b7 Build: Platform Choice<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Choosing How to Build<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">There are three categories of build approach. The right one depends on your team’s technical capability, not on which sounds most sophisticated. The goal is a working agent in production, not an impressive architecture diagram.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"build-grid\" style=\"margin-top:32px;\">\n <div class=\"build-card\">\n <div class=\"build-main\">\n <div class=\"build-title\">Start Narrow, Not Broad<\/div>\n <p class=\"build-body\">A Level 1 agent has one trigger, one primary flow, and one output. Resist scope expansion during build. Every feature added before the base case works is a failure mode that is harder to debug. Build the happy path first. Add edge case handling second. Add features third.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"build-tool\">\n <span class=\"tool-label\">Tool Category<\/span>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">No-Code<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">Architect, n8n, Relevance AI. Best for teams without a dedicated developer. Visual workflow builders with pre-built integrations. See <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/n8n-alternative\/\">how workflow tools compare to agent platforms<\/a>.<\/div><\/div>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">SDK<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">LangChain, LlamaIndex, CrewAI. Best for teams with a developer who can write Python. More control, more setup. See <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/crew-ai-alternative\/\">CrewAI alternative<\/a> and <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/how-langchain-inspired-lyzrs-journey-to-become-a-full-stack-generative-ai-stack-for-enterprises\/\">how LangChain inspired Lyzr’s full-stack approach<\/a>.<\/div><\/div>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">Raw API<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">Direct API calls to OpenAI, Anthropic, etc. Best for production-grade systems where you need full control. Highest engineering overhead. Or use the <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/agent-api\/\">Lyzr Agent API<\/a> for direct programmatic access without rebuilding the orchestration layer.<\/div><\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"build-platform\">\n <span class=\"platform-label\">On Architect (architect.new)<\/span>\n <p class=\"build-body\" style=\"font-size:13px;\">Use Guided Mode to generate your Plan Document and agent architecture from your Design Canvas. The platform selects models and populates prompts from enterprise templates. Recommended for teams without dedicated AI engineering.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n\n <div class=\"build-card\">\n <div class=\"build-main\">\n <div class=\"build-title\">Write the SLO Before the Code<\/div>\n <p class=\"build-body\">Define “working” in numbers before a single line is written. “Error rate below 3% on first-pass resolution. Latency under 8 seconds P95. Volume threshold: handles 100+ queries\/day without degradation.” These numbers are your exit criteria for Build and your entry criteria for Deploy.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"build-tool\">\n <span class=\"tool-label\">What SLO Monitoring Looks Like<\/span>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">No-Code<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">Most platforms have built-in run logging. Export logs to a spreadsheet or dashboard. Check weekly minimum.<\/div><\/div>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">SDK<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">LangSmith (LangChain), Helicone, or Langfuse for tracing. Add a logging wrapper to every agent call.<\/div><\/div>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">Raw API<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">Build your own logging layer. Log input, output, latency, and token count for every call. Store in your database of choice.<\/div><\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"build-platform\">\n <span class=\"platform-label\">On Architect (architect.new)<\/span>\n <p class=\"build-body\" style=\"font-size:13px;\">Push to Agents creates your agent architecture with locked-in prompts, KB integration, and tool connections. Run logs are visible in the platform. Edit agent parameters in Studio before deployment.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n\n <div class=\"build-card\">\n <div class=\"build-main\">\n <div class=\"build-title\">Test Before Trust<\/div>\n <p class=\"build-body\">Before any user-facing deployment: run 50+ test scenarios, including adversarial inputs. Test the handoff condition: confirm the agent actually escalates when it should. Test the failure mode: confirm the failure notification fires. A production agent with an untested handoff path is a liability, not a product.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"build-tool\">\n <span class=\"tool-label\">What Testing Looks Like<\/span>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">No-Code<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">Run test cases manually through the platform UI. Document pass\/fail. Use your 10 most common real inputs and your 5 most problematic edge cases.<\/div><\/div>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">SDK<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">Write a test suite using pytest or equivalent. Run it before every deployment. Add adversarial cases as you discover edge cases in production.<\/div><\/div>\n <div class=\"platform-row\"><div class=\"platform-name\">Raw API<\/div><div class=\"platform-desc\">Build an evaluation harness. Run the same 50 cases through every prompt iteration. Track pass rate across versions.<\/div><\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"build-platform\">\n <span class=\"platform-label\">On Architect (architect.new)<\/span>\n <p class=\"build-body\" style=\"font-size:13px;\">Use the Live Preview in the App tab to test agent responses in real time. Deploy to a staged URL on Netlify before sharing externally. The staged URL is your test environment.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- AGENT ARCHITECTURE PRIMER -->\n<section class=\"section-cream-alt\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 03 \u00b7 Build: How It Works<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Agent Architecture:<br>The Four Components<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Every agent, regardless of platform or framework, is made of four components. Understanding what each one does tells you what to configure, what to test, and what breaks when something goes wrong.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"arch-grid\">\n <div class=\"arch-card\">\n <div class=\"arch-ghost\">LLM<\/div>\n <span class=\"arch-num\">Component 01<\/span>\n <div class=\"arch-title\">The LLM (The Brain)<\/div>\n <p class=\"arch-body\">The model that reasons, plans, and decides what to do next. It reads the input, retrieves from memory if needed, chooses which tools to call, and generates the output. The LLM does not take actions directly. It decides what actions to take, then the orchestration layer executes them.<\/p>\n <div class=\"arch-example\">What you configure: model choice, system prompt, temperature, max tokens, and stop conditions.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"arch-card\">\n <div class=\"arch-ghost\">MEM<\/div>\n <span class=\"arch-num\">Component 02<\/span>\n <div class=\"arch-title\">Memory<\/div>\n <p class=\"arch-body\">What the agent knows and remembers. Short-term memory holds the current session: the conversation so far, retrieved documents, intermediate results. Long-term memory persists across sessions: customer history, learned preferences, past outcomes. Most first agents need only short-term memory. Add long-term memory only when you can measure the quality improvement it provides. Deep dive: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/how-to-build-a-state-of-the-art-sota-rag-engine\/\">building a state-of-the-art RAG engine<\/a> and <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/agentic-rag\/\">agentic RAG<\/a>.<\/p>\n <div class=\"arch-example\">What you configure: context window size, retrieval method (RAG, vector search, SQL lookup), what gets stored and for how long.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"arch-card\">\n <div class=\"arch-ghost\">TOOL<\/div>\n <span class=\"arch-num\">Component 03<\/span>\n <div class=\"arch-title\">Tools<\/div>\n <p class=\"arch-body\">The list of actions the agent can take. Each tool is a function with a name, a description, and an input\/output schema. The LLM reads the tool descriptions and decides which one to call based on the task. Tools are what turn a chatbot into an agent. Without tools, the agent can only generate text. With tools, it can search a database, send an email, update a CRM record, or call any API you have defined. The modern standard for tool definition: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/mcp-in-lyzr-agent-studio\/\">MCP in Lyzr Agent Studio<\/a>.<\/p>\n <div class=\"arch-example\">What you configure: tool name, description (the LLM reads this to decide when to use it), input parameters, output format, and error handling.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"arch-card\">\n <div class=\"arch-ghost\">ORCH<\/div>\n <span class=\"arch-num\">Component 04<\/span>\n <div class=\"arch-title\">The Run Loop (Orchestration)<\/div>\n <p class=\"arch-body\">The engine that keeps the agent working. It runs a cycle: observe the current state, reason about what to do next, take an action, check if the goal is reached. It continues until either the goal is complete, a stop condition fires (max steps, error threshold), or the agent decides to hand off to a human. The run loop is also where retry logic, error handling, and fallback behavior live. A well-designed run loop is the difference between an agent that fails gracefully and one that fails silently. Architectural comparisons: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/multi-agent-architecture\/\">multi-agent architecture<\/a> and <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/orchestration-templates-in-lyzr-agent-studio\/\">orchestration templates<\/a>.<\/p>\n <div class=\"arch-example\">What you configure: max iterations, retry logic, fallback model, stop conditions, and handoff triggers.<\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- FIRST 48 HOURS -->\n<section class=\"section-cream\">\n <div class=\"section-inner\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 03 \u00b7 Build: Getting Started<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Your First 48 Hours<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">This is the section that converts a completed Design Canvas into something running. Follow these steps in order. Do not skip to step 4 because it sounds more interesting. The point of steps 1\u20133 is to eliminate variables before you add complexity.<\/p>\n\n <ul class=\"steps-list\" style=\"margin-top:28px;\">\n <li class=\"step-item\">\n <div class=\"step-num\">1<\/div>\n <div class=\"step-content\">\n <div class=\"step-title\">Get API access to your chosen model<\/div>\n <p class=\"step-body\">Create an account with your model provider (OpenAI, Anthropic, or your platform of choice). Get an API key. Set a spending limit before you do anything else: set it low, around $20. You will hit it and need to raise it. You will never accidentally spend $500 on a misconfigured loop.<\/p>\n <p class=\"step-note\">If you are using a no-code platform like Architect, create your account and run one of the default example agents before building your own. Confirm it works end-to-end before touching your use case.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/li>\n <li class=\"step-item\">\n <div class=\"step-num\">2<\/div>\n <div class=\"step-content\">\n <div class=\"step-title\">Build a version with no tools and no integrations<\/div>\n <p class=\"step-body\">Write a system prompt that describes your agent’s role and goal. Send it a real example input from your use case. Look at the output. Is the reasoning coherent? Is the format correct? Fix the prompt until you get output you would be comfortable showing a user. This step has zero integration risk and tells you how hard your prompt engineering job is going to be.<\/p>\n <p class=\"step-note\">Time to complete: 2\u20134 hours. If it takes longer, your task is more complex than your Design Canvas suggests. Revisit section 08 (SLO) before continuing.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/li>\n <li class=\"step-item\">\n <div class=\"step-num\">3<\/div>\n <div class=\"step-content\">\n <div class=\"step-title\">Add one tool. Test it in isolation.<\/div>\n <p class=\"step-body\">Add the first tool from your Design Canvas section 04. Call it manually with a test input. Confirm the output is what you expect. Then add it to the agent and run the same test input you used in step 2. Confirm the agent uses the tool correctly and that the output improves. Add tools one at a time. Never add two at once.<\/p>\n <p class=\"step-note\">The most common mistake: adding all tools at once, getting a failure, and not knowing which tool caused it.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/li>\n <li class=\"step-item\">\n <div class=\"step-num\">4<\/div>\n <div class=\"step-content\">\n <div class=\"step-title\">Test your handoff condition before anything else goes to production<\/div>\n <p class=\"step-body\">Before adding more tools or more complexity: deliberately trigger the handoff condition from your Design Canvas section 07. Send an input that should cause escalation. Confirm the agent escalates correctly and that the right person is notified. This is the most commonly skipped test and the most consequential one.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/li>\n <li class=\"step-item\">\n <div class=\"step-num\">5<\/div>\n <div class=\"step-content\">\n <div class=\"step-title\">Run 20 real inputs, document every failure<\/div>\n <p class=\"step-body\">Pull 20 real examples from your use case (not invented test cases). Run them through the agent. For every failure, note what went wrong: wrong tool call, wrong format, wrong reasoning, or missed handoff trigger. Fix the most common failure pattern before adding more test cases. Repeat until pass rate is above 80% on 20 cases, then scale to 50.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/li>\n <li class=\"step-item\">\n <div class=\"step-num\">6<\/div>\n <div class=\"step-content\">\n <div class=\"step-title\">Deploy to a staged URL and test with 3 internal users<\/div>\n <p class=\"step-body\">Get the agent running at a URL that real users can access. Ask 3 people from the target team to use it for one week with real inputs. Collect every failure. Fix the top 3 most common issues. Only after this step should you consider production deployment.<\/p>\n <p class=\"step-note\">On Architect: use the Deploy button to get a live Netlify URL. Share it with your internal testers before announcing it broadly.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/li>\n <li class=\"step-item\">\n <div class=\"step-num\">7<\/div>\n <div class=\"step-content\">\n <div class=\"step-title\">Activate governance before opening to more users<\/div>\n <p class=\"step-body\">Before more than 5 people have access: turn on audit logging, set your cost cap, configure RBAC, and confirm the SLO monitoring dashboard is live. These are not optional extras to add later. They are Phase Gate 3 requirements. If they are not active, you are not ready to deploy broadly.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- COMMON FIRST AGENT MISTAKES -->\n<section class=\"section-cream-alt\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 03 \u00b7 Build: What to Avoid<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Common First Agent Mistakes<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">These are the patterns that consistently kill agents between demo and production. They are not edge cases. They are the norm.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"mistakes-grid\">\n <div class=\"mistake-card\">\n <div class=\"mistake-icon\">\u2715<\/div>\n <div>\n <div class=\"mistake-title\">Context windows that overflow on real data<\/div>\n <p class=\"mistake-body\">Your prompt works perfectly on a 200-word test input and breaks silently on a 2,000-word real document. Set a hard limit on input size and add a truncation or chunking step before the agent runs. Test with your longest real inputs, not your shortest.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"mistake-card\">\n <div class=\"mistake-icon\">\u2715<\/div>\n <div>\n <div class=\"mistake-title\">Prompts that work in testing and break on edge cases<\/div>\n <p class=\"mistake-body\">The model does exactly what your prompt says. The problem is that your prompt describes what to do in the happy path, not what to do when inputs are ambiguous, incomplete, or adversarial. Add explicit instructions for what the agent should do when it is uncertain. “If you cannot determine X with confidence, escalate” beats leaving it to the model’s judgment.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"mistake-card\">\n <div class=\"mistake-icon\">\u2715<\/div>\n <div>\n <div class=\"mistake-title\">Tools that work in isolation but fail when chained<\/div>\n <p class=\"mistake-body\">Tool A works. Tool B works. When the agent calls Tool A and passes its output to Tool B, the output format from A does not match the input format B expects, and the whole chain fails. Always test the full sequence end-to-end, not just individual tools. Define the input and output schema for every tool explicitly and test handoffs between them.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"mistake-card\">\n <div class=\"mistake-icon\">\u2715<\/div>\n <div>\n <div class=\"mistake-title\">Auth tokens that expire after 30 days<\/div>\n <p class=\"mistake-body\">You authenticate, the agent works, you deploy. 30 days later, the auth token expires and the agent fails on every call with a cryptic error. Implement token refresh logic before production or set a calendar reminder to rotate tokens before they expire. The first expiry usually happens when you are not watching.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"mistake-card\">\n <div class=\"mistake-icon\">\u2715<\/div>\n <div>\n <div class=\"mistake-title\">No structured output enforcement<\/div>\n <p class=\"mistake-body\">You ask the model to return JSON. It returns JSON most of the time. On 3% of calls, it wraps the JSON in markdown backticks, adds an explanation before it, or returns slightly different field names. Your downstream system breaks. Use structured output mode (function calling \/ JSON mode) where available. Never parse free-text output with regex in production.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"mistake-card\">\n <div class=\"mistake-icon\">\u2715<\/div>\n <div>\n <div class=\"mistake-title\">No retry logic on transient failures<\/div>\n <p class=\"mistake-body\">API calls fail. Networks have timeouts. Model providers have brief outages. An agent with no retry logic treats a 500ms network hiccup the same as a genuine error. Implement exponential backoff with a maximum of 3 retries on any external API call. Log every retry. Alert on any call that exhausts all retries.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"mistake-card\">\n <div class=\"mistake-icon\">\u2715<\/div>\n <div>\n <div class=\"mistake-title\">Real documents are messier than sample documents<\/div>\n <p class=\"mistake-body\">You test with 5 clean PDFs. Production has 500 PDFs, 30% of which are scanned images with no text layer, have tables in non-standard formats, or are password-protected. Test your document processing pipeline on your 20 messiest real documents before build is complete. If any fail, solve that before deployment.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"mistake-card\">\n <div class=\"mistake-icon\">\u2715<\/div>\n <div>\n <div class=\"mistake-title\">Skipping the handoff test<\/div>\n <p class=\"mistake-body\">The agent handles the happy path in testing. Nobody tests whether the escalation path actually works. The first time a production case should escalate, the escalation fails silently and the customer gets no response. Test your handoff condition before anything else goes to production. It is step 4 in the First 48 Hours section for this reason.<\/p>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- PHASE 4 GOVERN -->\n<section class=\"section-dark\" id=\"govern\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <div class=\"phase-badge\"><span class=\"phase-badge-dot\"><\/span> Phase 04 \u00b7 Deploy & Govern<\/div>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Governance: The Layer That<br>Determines Whether Agents Scale<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\">Governance is not a compliance checkbox. It is the mechanism by which agents earn the organizational trust needed to expand. Build it before you need it. By the time you need it, it is already too late to add it cleanly.<\/p>\n\n <table class=\"gov-table\" style=\"margin-top:40px;\">\n <thead><tr><th>Layer<\/th><th>What It Is<\/th><th>Minimum Requirement<\/th><\/tr><\/thead>\n <tbody>\n <tr><td>RBAC<\/td><td>Role-Based Access Control<\/td><td>Four roles defined before deployment: Admin, Operator, Viewer, Override. No agent accessible without authentication.<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Audit Trail<\/td><td>Every action, logged<\/td><td>Every agent action logged with timestamp, agent ID, input received, output produced, and any human override. Logs retained minimum 90 days.<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Cost Caps<\/td><td>Per-run and monthly limits<\/td><td>Maximum cost per agent run + monthly budget ceiling. Alert at >2x normal token consumption per session.<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Approval Gates<\/td><td>Human-in-the-loop for high stakes<\/td><td>Any irreversible action (external communication, financial record modification, data deletion) requires human approval until error rate is below SLO for 30+ consecutive days.<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>SLO Monitoring<\/td><td>Numbers, not feelings<\/td><td>Monitor: error rate (% of runs with incorrect output), latency (P95 response time), volume throughput. Alert at 1.5x baseline. Escalate at 2x.<\/td><\/tr>\n <tr><td>Prompt Versioning<\/td><td>Change control for AI<\/td><td>Every change to prompt, model, or tool configuration version-controlled with timestamp and author. Rollback possible within 10 minutes. No prompt changes in production without signed review.<\/td><\/tr>\n <\/tbody>\n <\/table>\n <p class=\"body-text-dark\" style=\"margin-top:32px;\">The governance layer above is delivered through Lyzr’s control-plane services: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/agent-eval-as-a-service\/\">agent evaluation<\/a>, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/knowledge-graph-as-a-service\/\">knowledge graph<\/a>, <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/hallucination-manager-as-a-service\/\">hallucination management<\/a>, and <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/responsible-ai-as-a-service\/\">responsible AI<\/a>. For RBAC + audit configuration in practice: <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/blog\/governance-in-aws\/\">Governance in AWS<\/a>.<\/p>\n <div style=\"margin-top:24px; text-align:center;\">\n <a href=\"https:\/\/www.lyzr.ai\/book-demo\/?utm_source=roadmap&utm_medium=inline_cta&utm_campaign=governance\" style=\"display:inline-block; background:#d97706; color:#fff; padding:14px 32px; border-radius:8px; font-weight:600; text-decoration:none; font-size:14px;\">See the governance stack on your environment \u2192 Book a demo<\/a>\n <\/div>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- PRE-DEPLOYMENT CHECKLIST -->\n<section class=\"section-cream\">\n <div class=\"section-inner\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 04 \u00b7 Deploy: Pre-Flight<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">Pre-Deployment Checklist<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">Every box must be checked before production deployment. If any box remains unchecked, it is a risk, not a detail.<\/p>\n <ul class=\"preflight-list\" style=\"margin-top:28px;\">\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Agent Design Canvas complete and signed by champion and project lead<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Integration Map: all data sources confirmed with API access and auth<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> SLOs defined in writing: error rate, latency, volume thresholds<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> RBAC configured: all four roles defined and assigned<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Audit logging active: test log entries verified<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Cost caps set: per-run limit and monthly ceiling configured<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Approval gates configured for all irreversible actions<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> 50+ test scenarios run including adversarial inputs<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Handoff condition tested: agent escalates correctly<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Failure notification tested: alert fires correctly<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Staged deployment URL tested by 3+ internal users<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Rollback procedure documented and tested<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> Auth token expiry dates noted and refresh schedule set<\/li>\n <li class=\"preflight-item\"><input type=\"checkbox\"> SLO monitoring dashboard live and verified<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <\/div>\n<\/section>\n\n<!-- 30-DAY MONITORING \u2014 NEW SECTION -->\n<section class=\"section-cream-alt\" id=\"monitoring\">\n <div class=\"section-inner-wide\">\n <span class=\"eyebrow-dark\">Phase 04 \u00b7 Deploy: What Comes Next<\/span>\n <h2 class=\"section-title\">What a Real Agent Looks Like<br>at 30 Days<\/h2>\n <p class=\"body-text\">The most common unknown for first-time builders is not how to build the agent. It is how to know whether the agent is working after deployment. This section tells you what a healthy agent looks like, what a struggling one looks like, and what to do about the difference.<\/p>\n\n <div class=\"monitor-grid\">\n <div class=\"monitor-card\">\n <span class=\"monitor-label\">Week 1<\/span>\n <div class=\"monitor-title\">Establish Baseline<\/div>\n <ul class=\"monitor-items\">\n <li>Log every run. Review logs daily, not weekly.<\/li>\n <li>Record actual error rate, latency, and volume<\/li>\n <li>Note every case that escalated to a human and why<\/li>\n <li>Document every case that should have escalated but didn’t<\/li>\n <li>Compare actual cost per run to estimate from business case<\/li>\n <li>Your SLO targets are aspirational at this stage. Your job is measurement, not optimization.<\/li>\n <\/ul>\n <\/div>\n <div class=\"monitor-card\">\n <span class=\"monitor-label\">Weeks 2\u20133<\/span>\n <div class=\"monitor-title\">Identify Patterns<\/div>\n <ul class=\"monitor-items\">\n <li>Categorize failure modes: are they prompt failures, tool failures, or integration failures?<\/li>\n <li>Identify the most common input type that causes failures<\/li>\n <li>Check whether escalation rate is trending up or down<\/li>\n <li>Confirm cost per run is stable and predictable<\/li>\n <li>Fix the top 2 failure patterns. Version-control every change.<\/li>\n <li>Do not fix more than 2 things at once.
